ISSUE NO. 1 

To prove this issue Mr. A. R. Barthakur, the learned counsel 
on behalf of the Governments, relics on the Evidence of Wit¬ 
nesses No. 1, 2 and 3 and on Exhibits P|3 to P|22, P|26 to 
P|38 and P|40. 

It is staled in the Resume (Ext. P|40) that the Meitei Ex¬ 
tremist Organisations continue to exist as ‘live orgenisaiions’ 
having declared as their objective the formation of an inde¬ 
pendent Manipur comprhing the State of Manipur and bring¬ 
ing about secession of the said State from the Union of India 
and that they continue to employ aimed fo-'-cev to attack security 
forces, civilian Government and citizens in the State of Mani¬ 
pur and indulge in acts of looting, intimidation and collection 
of fundi) for their Organisations: and further that efforts have 
also been made by them lo resume contacts with foreign coun¬ 
tries for securing financial assistance and assistance by way 
of arms training. Witness No. 2, M. V. Natarajan, who is the 
Director in the Miniatry of Home Affairs, Government of 
India, New Delhi and in that capacity looks after Ihc papers 
^rtaining to lavv and order in the whole of North Eastern 
Region, except Ass^, and, as such, fully conversant with 
the full facts and circum.stnn'"cs leading lo the issue of the 
Notification, deposes tha( the facts mentioned bv him in the 
Resunje are, to the best of hia knowledge and belief, true. He 
states that in 1979 the Government had issued a Notification 
declaring certain Meitei Extremist organisations as cmlawful 
associations and the said Notification was confirmed. At that 
time it waa expected that the various Meitei Extremist Organisa¬ 
tions would cease to indulge in violence. The expectation, 
however, did not come true. Instead, there was a spurt in 
violent activities and the State Government had to declare 


the whole of Iniphal Valley ar disturbed aiea under the 
Armed Forces (Special Powers) Act in September, 1980, Wit¬ 
ness No. I, Soubam Ibppishak Singh, is a Deputy Superin¬ 
tendent of Police, poste'd in the Criminal Invcucigation De¬ 
partment III Manipur. He is by birth Munipuii, belongs to 
the Meitei community, was bom, brought up and educated 
in Manipur, and in fully convorsant with the Manipuri langu¬ 
age. He is also fully conversant with the local conditions 
including the law and order situation in the .State of Manipur. 
He deposes that the Meitei Extremist Organisations have 
ojienly declared theh objective to be the establishment of 
a Hovereign State of Manipur .ind to bung about secession 
of the Sate of Manipur from the Union of India, and 
that these extiemist organisations have also employed their 
aimed forces toi achieve their objective. Witness No, 3, 
Kh. Mohendra Singh, is the Under Secretary to the Govein- 
meii of Man pur in the Home Department In hb affidavit 
(Ext. P|41) he states that the Meitci Extremiat Organisation* 
have openly declared their objective to be the lormation of 
an independent Manipur comprising the .State of Manipur 
and have resorted to violent activities in pursuance of their 
objective lo bring about sMicssion of the said State from 
the Union of India and to achieve their objective, have 
employed their armed forces in attacking security forces and 
the civil Government. 

T he above statements are alsio corroborated by the con¬ 
tents of Dawn, Volume 1, 1979 (Ext. P|3), Dawn, Volume II, 
1979 (Ext. P)4), and Dawn, Volume III, 19.80 (Ext. P|5,'* 
published by the PLA wherein giving oblique interpretation* 
to communist ideology and terminology, the Government 
of India is sought to be depicted as their enemy, and the 
expressions like “Ddhi-bandits”. “Common enemy”, ‘Delhi- 
Mayangs”, and “Mayang Administration” have been used. 
In Dawn, Vohunc II, contains the communist song “In¬ 
ternationale” and the following slogans appear : (1) Down 
with Reactionaries, (2) Down with people's enemy, (3) Down 
with Delhi gangs, (4) Down with Mayang regime, (5) Down 
with Delhi-puppet (Running dogs), (6) P.L.A. Defends our 
motherland, (7) P.L.A. Army of the people, (8) We support 
People’.s Liberation Army, (9) We want Independence, and 
(10) We want Liberation. 

According lo Witness No. 1, ‘Mayang’ in Manipuii means 
a person who is not born and brought up in Manipur. In 
the leaflet (Ext. P|1I2) captioned-“REVOLT WE MUST” 
the expressions “Imperialist India", “We must raise arms” 
to defeat the “Imperialist Govt, of India" are found. It says ; 
‘‘For waging war against the enemy it is necessary to take 
help fiom other country of the world. They will aluo help 
us against the imperialist of InJia" Ext. P|ll is captioned 
‘WAR BULLETIN FOR INDEPENDENCE" published by 
the CON.SOCOM and PREPAK where it had, inter alia, dec¬ 
lared that the party would continue to sluigglc for the in 
dependence of Mampur from India ‘Imperialists' end their 
‘watch dogs and that it would annihilate the ‘pet dogs’ of 
‘imperialist India’ foi achieving independence. Ex. P'33 is a 
leaflet published by the K.C.P. which, according to it, has 
been formed "only to encounter from the supiession of the 
Govt, of India, to relieve us fiom their rule and to bring 
equality and freedom from the rule of a foreigner.” 

From the above evidence there can be no doubt that the 
Meitei Extremist Organisations have openly declared as 
their objective the formation of nii independent Manipur com¬ 
prising the State of Manipur and have resorted to violent 
activities in pursuance of their objective to bring about 
secession of the State of Manipur from the Union of India, 

ISSUE NO. 2

ISSl.'t NO. 6 

Section 2 (f) of the Act defines '“unlawful activity” asunder. 

“(f) ‘unlwaful activity', in iJa ^on at an individual of 
aasocitation, means any action taken by such indivi¬ 
dual or association (whether by committing an act 
or by woids, eiihcr spoken or v ,ittcn, or by signs 
or by visible ropreseniatio,-i oi otherwise), 

(i) which is intended, or supiioits any claim, to bring 

about, on any giound vvhatsoevcr, the cession of 
a part of the territory of India or the secession of 
a part of the territory of India from the Union oi 
which incite.s any individual or group of individuals 
to bring about such ce^ision of secession; 

(ii) which disclaims, questions, disiupts or intended to 

disrupt the sovereignty and territorial integrity of 
India." 

The evidence discussed under the foregoing issue leaves no 
doubt that the Meitei Extremist Origanisations have been in¬ 
dulging in unlawful activities, as defined in the Act, 


of India, enjoins that it shalj be the duty of every citizen of 
India to abide by the Constitution; to uphold and piotect the 
sovereignty, unity and integrity of India; to defend the security 
ot the State; to dotend the country and render national ser¬ 
vice when called upon to do so; to promote harmony and the 
spirit of (jomraon brotherhood amongst all the (leople of India 
transcoding roligious, lingiistic and regional or sectional di¬ 
versities; to renoiuice piactices deicgatory to the dignity of 
women; to safeguard public property: and to adjure violence. 
Secession of any Stale fioni the Union of India is not coun¬ 
tenanced by the Constitution of India. Seemity of the State 
means the absence of serious aggravated lor.m of public dis¬ 
order, and it is endangered by Crimea of violence intended 
to overthrow the Government, levy ot war and relidiation 
against the Government, external aggression or large scale 
unlawful activities. Tile sovereign State is the fountain of all 
lights. To art against the sovereignty and integrity of the 
Slate is to act against the fundamental rights of all the citizens. 
Evoi-y coimtiy and age has iis ovvn problems. Anciept Hindu 
luiists emphasised the concepts of “Desha" (country) and 
“‘haia" (tinm). The country is presently facing the peculiar 
unlawful problem in its North Eaatcin Region. Scrutton L.J. 
is said to have observed in Rounfeldt Vs. Phillips (1918) 33 
TLR 46, that « war could not be carried according to the 
Sermon on the Mount, Similarly it was observed that a war 
could not be cairied on acvoiding to the p:inciplcs of Magna 
C.iiia. Very wide powers have been given to the executive to 
Act on suspicion on matters aff 'cting (he interest of the S’ate, 
It may be nccesvuy oi expedient foi security and publi'’ s.sfety, 
sovereignly and the integrity of the country, and the main¬ 
tenance of public order that the executive be given the power 
to act according to law as and When it considers expedient. 
The Act is such a piece of legislation to provide for the more 
effective prevention of unlawful activities of individuals and 
associations; and the oxocntive has to be allowed reasonable 
discretion in such matters with du© regard to the fundamental 
rights and fundamental duties enshrined in the Constitution 
of India. 


ISSUEE NO. 7 

“Unlawful nssoi'i.ition’ has been defined under Section 2(g) 
of the Act as follows :—■ 

“(g) “unlawful as-sociation’ means any association 

(i) Which has for its object any unlawful activity, oi 
which encourages or aids persons to undertake any 
unlawful activity, or of which the members under¬ 
take such activity; or 

(ii) Which has for its object any activity which is punish¬ 
able under Sec, 153-A or Ecc, 153-n of the Indian 
Panel Code (45 of 1860) or which encourages or 
aids persons to undertake any such activity, or of 
which the members undertake any such activity. 

Provided...” 

The Moitei Extremist Organisations having indulged in un¬ 
lawful activities as di.scussed under (he foregoing hues, have 
become unlawful associations. 


The Notification is unubtedly a pieveiifive measure. Whcit 
urgency in the interest of Public security, sivereignty and th- 
tegrity of the country may warrant, disregard of the alteram 
partem Rule is generally conceded. Preventive action under 
the pioviso to sub-sectlnii (3) of Section 3 of the Act may, 
therefore, be Justified. Undei y\rti-le 19 of the Constitution of 
India all citizers sliall .bine right to freedom of 
speech and expressions; to assemble peaceably and without 
arm.<); and to form associations o- unions. These rights, how¬ 


ever, shall not affect the opei aiion of any exislini} law in so 
farzaa it imposes, or prevent the Stale from making any law 
imposing in the interest of the sovereignty and integrity of 
In^a Or public ordei-, reasonable restrictions on the exercise 
of the above rights, The Unlawful Activities (Prevention) Act 
w an Act to provide for the more effective prevention of 
certain unlavvful activities of individuals and associations and 
for m.ifters conpee'ed thcre'vith. While considering the funda- 
monial rlj^t to form associations and to assemble peaceably 
without arms one has to consider similar fundamental rights 
of other citizens, “Salus Populi Supreme Lei’, the safety of 
the people is the supreme law. .Article 5IA of the Constitution 


Considering the credible evidence on record, including the 
affidavits on behalf of the Union of India and the Govern- 
rnent of Manipur and on pioper adjudication of the Notifica¬ 
tion referred, I consider it safe to conclude that there is 
sufficient cause for declaring the Meilei Extremist Oraginsa- 
tions as unlawful associations. I accordingly deem it fit 
and confirm the declaration made in the Nofincation, 

The declaration made in the Noiiflcaiion i.s confirmed and 
tile Reference is accepted.

sclenoes subject to the following conditions;— 

(i) That the Jagdale Scientific Research Foundation, 
Bangalore will maintain a separate account of the 
sums received by it for scientific research in the 
• field of natural and applied sciences other than

cines ; 

(il) That the said Foundation will furnish annual re¬ 
turns of its soientifle research activities to ^e Pres¬ 
cribed Authority for every financial year in such 
forms as may be laid down and Intimated to them 
for this purpose by 30th April, each year. 

(lii) That the said Foundation will submit their Annual 
accounts showing their total income and expenditure 
as also a copy of their Balance Sheet showing its 
assets and liabilities to the concerned Income-tax

Whereas the Income-tax Circle, Ward or District or part 
thereof stands transferred by this Notification from one Range 
to another ranges, appeal arising out of the assessments made 
in that Income-tax Circle, Ward or Districts or part thereof and 
pending immediately before the date of this Notification before 
the Appellate Assistant Commissioner of the Range from whom 
that Income-tax Circle, Ward and Districts or part thereof 
is transferred shall from the date of this Notification takes effect 
be transferred to and dealt with by the Appellate Assistant Co¬ 
mmissioner of tne Range to whom the said Circle, Ward or 
Districts or part thereof is transferred. 

This Notification shall take effect from 1(3-11-981.

Office of tbe Joint Chief Controller of Imports and Exports 
(CENTRAL LICENSING AREA) 

cancellation order 

New Delhi, the 5th April. 1982. 

5. O, 1768:—M/s. Kehr Surgical & Allied Products (P) 
Ltd., B-4I, Site No. 4, Industrial Area, Sahibabad, District 
Ohaziabad (U. P.) were granted Import Licence No. P/L/0316649 
dated 23-2-82 for Rs. 12,35,047 for import of the fbllowing 


items :— 

Rs. 

1. High Carbon Steel Strips. 36.39 M.T. 10,14,275 

2. VPI Paper Rolls 2.00 M.T. 10,63,00 

3. Irradiation Indicator Labels 110000 PCS 7,000 

4. Felt Polishing Wheels 240 PCS 47,520 

5. Grinding Wheels 120 PCS 24,720 

6 . Polishing Paste 430 Kgs. 10,836 

7. Ultrasonic Cleaning Material 1200 Kgs. 24,396 


The firm have reported that both Customs Purpose Copy 
as Well as Exchange Control Copy of the same have been lost/ 
misplaced without having been registered with any Custom 
Authorities and utilised at all. 

2. The applicant firm have filed an Affidavit in support 
of the above statement as required under paras 352-354 of Hand 


BooV of Imports and Export Procedure. 1981-82, I am satis¬ 
fied that the Original Customs Purpose Copy and Exchange 
Control Copy of the said licence have been lost/misplaced. 

3. In exercise of the powers conferred on me under Section 
9{cc) of Import Trade Control Order 1955 dt. 7-12-1955 as 
anoended, I order the cancellation of the said Original Custom 
Copy and Exchange Control Copy of the said licence. 

4. The applicant's case will now be considered for the issue 
of Duplicate Licence (Custom Copy and Exchange Control 
Copy) in accordance with para 352-354 of Hand Book of Rules 
& Procedure 1981-82. 

[No. ADV/LIC/UDES/255/AM-82/EP-VI/CLA/I] 

S. BALAKRISHNA PILLAI, Dy. Chief Controller 
of Imports & Exports

S.O. 1774.—^Whereas by a notification of the Government 
of India in the Ministrv of Petroleum. Chemicals & Ferti¬ 
lizer (Department of Petioleuni), S. O. No. 1572 dated 5-5-81 
under sub-section (1) of sectian 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land) 
Act. 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government declared 
Its intention to acaiiirc the right of user in the lands speci¬ 
fied in the schedule appended to that notification for the 
purpose of laying pipeline ; 

And further In ex-eredse of power conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of Section 6 of the! said Act. submitted report 
to the Government; 

And Further whereas the Central Government has after 
considering the said report, decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the 
said lands specified in Ihe schedule appended to this noti¬ 
fication hereby 'acquired for laying the pipline; 

And, further in exercise of power conferred by sub¬ 
section (4) of that section, the Central Government directs 
that the right of user in the said lands shall^ instead of 
vesting in the Central Government vest on this date of the 
pubifeation of the declaration in the Oil & Natural Ghs 
Commission fsce fioin encumbrances.

S.O. 1776.—Whereas by noUflcation of the Goverument of 
India rn the Ministry of Petroleum, Chemicata & Fertilizer, 
(Department of Petroleum) S. O. 2790 dated 20-9-81 under 
sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines (Acqultion of Right of User in Land) Act, 1962 
(50 of 1962), the Central Government declared its intention 
to acquire the right of user m the lands specified m the 
schedule appended to that notification for the purpose of 
laying pipeline ; 

And Whereas the Competent Authority has under sub¬ 
section (ll) of Section 6 of the said Act, submitted report 
to the (jovermnent; 

And Further whereas the Central Government has after 
considering the said report, decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands specified In the schedule appended to this 
notification; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user m the 
said lands specified in the schedule appended to this nMi- 
fication hereby acquired for laying the pipline; 

And further in exercise of power conferred by sub¬ 
section (4) of that section, the Central Government directs 
that the rl^t of users in the said lands shall instead of 
vesting in tne Central Government vest on this date of tJu; 
publication of the declaration in the Oil Natural OH 

Commission free from encumbrances.

S.O. 1779.—^Whereas by notification of the Government of 
India in the Ministry of Petroleum, Chemicals & Fertilizer, 
(Department of Petroleum) S.O. 194 dated 6th lanuary, 1982 
under sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land) 
Act. 1962 (50 of 1962). the Central Government declared 
its intention to acquire the right of user in the lands specified 
in the schedule appended to that notification for the purpose 
of laying pipeline; 



[«inrll—3(ii)] 


1963 


tTTOTtn : »lt is, 1082 /^^ 25, 1904 


And whereas Ihe Competent Authority has under Sub¬ 
section (1) of Section 6 of the said Act, submitted report 
to the Government; 

And further whereas the Central Government has, after 
considering the said report, decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notifioation; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the 
said lands specified in the schedule appended to this notifica¬ 
tion hereby acquired for laying the pipeline ; 

And further in exercise of power conferred by sub-section 
(4) of that section, the Central Government directs that the 
right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in the 
Central Government vests on this date of the publication of 
this declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas Commission free 
from encumbrances.

S.O. 1780.—Whereas by notification of the Government of 
India m the Ministry of Petroleum, Chemicals & Fertilizer, 
(Department of Petroleum) S.O. 195 dated 6th lanuary, 1982 
under sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land)

its intention to acquire the right of user in the lands specified 
in the s Jiedule appended to that notification for the pur¬ 
pose of laying pipeline; 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under Sub¬ 
section (1) of Section 6 of the said Act, submitted report 
to the Government; 

And further whereas the Central Government has, after 
considerina the said report, decided to acquire the right of 
user in tte lands specified in the schedule appended to 
this notification; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the fScction 6 of the said Act, the Cenrtal 
Government hereby deoiores that the right of user in the said 
lands specified in the schedule appended to this notification 
hereby acquired for laying the pipeline; 

And further in exercise of power conferred by sub-section 
(4) of that section, the Central Government directs that the 
right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in the

this declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas Commission free 
from encumbrances.

S.O, 1782.—Whereas it appears tq the Cenlral Govern- 
mont that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of petroleum from South Santhal GGS to N. K. 
GGS I in Gujarat State pipeline should be laid by the Oil 
& Natural Gas Coatinission; 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it Is necessary to acquire the right of user 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and hfinerals 
pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) Act. 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares 
Its intention to acquire the right of user therein: 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to the 
laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent Autho¬ 
rity, Oil & Natural Gas Commission, Construction & Main¬ 
tenance Division, Makarpura Road, Vadodara (390009). 

And every person making such an objection sh.all also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be beared in person 
or by a legal practitioner.

S.O. 1783.—Whereas in pursuance of clause (e) of section 
3 read with sub-section (4) of section 6 of the Dentists Act, 
1948 (16 of 1948), Dr. S. D. Bhattacharya Professor and 
Head of the Deparlincnt of Dentistry, S. P. Medical College, 
Bikaner (Rajasthan), has been nominated to be member of 
the Dental Council of India by die Government of Rajasthan 
in place of Dr. S. D, Sharma with effect from the 18th 
January, 1982 to 18th September, 1982. 

Now, therefore. In pursuance of section 3 read with sub¬ 
section f4) of section 6 of the said Act, the Central Govern¬ 
ment hereby makes the following further amendments in the 
nodfleetion of the Government of India in the late Ministry 
of Health Na F. 10-10/48-MI, dated the 12di April, 1949, 
as republished b the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare, 
notification No S.O. 533. dated the 9th February, 1978, 
namely :— 

In the said notification, under heading "(0) Nombated 
under dause (e) of section 3**, for serial number 9 and the 
entries relating thereto, the foUowbg serial number and 
entries shall be substituted, najndy :— 

“Dr. S. D. Bhattacharya. Rajasthan 18-1-1982" 
Professor and Hoad of Government ‘CCE’

New Delhi, the 2Sth April, 1982 

S.O. 1785.—Whereas by the itotification of the Govern- 
rnebt cf India, in the Ministry of Energy, (Department of Coolt 
No. S.O 699 dated the 13th January, 1981, under sub-secticn (1) 
of Section 4 of the Coal Beniing Arens (Acquisition and Develop¬

notice of its intention to prospect for coal in 12,060.00 acres 
(approxim.itdy) or 4880.52 hectares (approximately) of the 
lands in the loctlity specified In the Schedule appended to that 
notification; 

AND WHEREAS the Central Government is satisfied 
that coal is obtRinrble in part of the said lands; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of Section 7 of the said Act, the Central Govern¬ 
ment hereby gives notice of its intention to acquire thelands- 
measurtng 11943.30 acres (approximately) or 4833 29 hec¬ 
tares (approximately) described in the Schedule appended 
hereto. 

Note I; The plan of the area covered by this notification may

Coalfields Limited. (Revenue Section), Dcrblvinga Home, 
Ranchi (Bih.ti), 

Note : 2 ; Attention is hereby invited to the provisions of 
Section 8 of the Cqal Bearing, Areas (Acquisition and Develop¬ 
ment) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957), which provides as follows ; 

“8(1) Any person interested in any lupd In respect of which, 
notification u idcr section 7 has been issued may 
within thirty days of tire issue of the notifleatk n, ob¬ 
ject to' the acquisition cf the whole or any part of the 
land or of any rights in or over such land. 

Explanation 1 It shall not be an objection within the meaning 
of this section for any person to say that he 
himself dcslfes to undertake mining operations 
111 the land fer the production of coal and that 
such operations should net be undertaken by 
the Central Govcrnnient or by any other person. 

(2> Every c bjection under Sub-section' (1) shall be made 
to the competent authority in writing, and the compe¬ 
tent authority shall give the objector an opportunity 
of being heard cither in person or by a legal practi¬ 
tioner and shall, after hearing all such objections and 
after making such further inquiry, if any, as he thinks 
necessary^ either make a reixirt in respect of the land 
which has'been notifioti under sub-Icction (1) of Section

S.O. 1788.—Whereas by the notification of the Govern¬ 
ment of India in the MinistF of F.ducation, Social Welfare 
and Culture, Archaeological Survey of India, S.O. No. 1048 
dated the 25th February, 1976, published in the Ciazette 
of India, Part II, Section 3, Sub-section (ii), dated the 13th 
March, 1976, at pages 1237 and 1238, the Central Govern¬ 
ment gave two months’ notice of its intention to declare 
the ancient monument specified in the Schedule annexed there¬ 
to, to be of national impoitance and inviting objections 
from persons likely to be affected thereby. 

And, whereas the owner filed an objection to the proposed 
declaration. 

The Central Government on consideration of the objection 
hereby withdraws the said notification issued under sub¬ 
section (1) of section 4 of tthe Ancient Monuments and 
Archaeologicial Sites and Remains Act, 1958 (24 of 1958).

MINISTRY OF SHIPPING AND TRANSPORT 
(Labour Division) 

New Delhi, the 27th April, 1982 

S.O. 1789.—^The following draft of a scheme further to 
amend the Calcutta Dock Workers (Regulation of Employ¬ 
ment) Scheme, 1970, which the Central Government pro¬ 
poses to make in exeicise of the powers conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of Section 4 of Dock Wlorkers (Regulation of 
Employment) Act, 1948 (9 of 1948), is hereby published as 
required by the said sub-seotion for the information of all 
persons likely to be affected thereby; and notice is hereby 
given that ihe .said draftwill be t iken ln‘o consideration on 
or after the expiry of a period of sixty days from the date 
of publication of this notification in the Official Gazette. 

Any objections or suggestions which may be received 
fiom any person with respect to the said draft before the 
aforesaid period will be considered by the Central Govern¬ 
ment. 

DRAFT SCHEME 

1. (1) This Scheme may be called the Calcutta Dock 

Workers (Regulation of Employment) Amendment 
Scheme, 1982. 

(2) It shall conic into force on the date of its final 
publication in the Official Gazette, 

2. In the Calcutta Dock Workeis (Regulation of Employ¬ 
ment) Scheme, 1970, in Schedule VI, for Note (1) occurring, 
under Item No. 4, Ihe following Note shall be substituted, 
namely ;— 

“(1) Individual Packages, weighing over 60 tonnes, shall 
be time-rated ; 

Provided that containers, irrespective of their ton¬ 
nage, shall be only time-rated.” 

[No, LDC/93/81-L. IV] 
V. SANKARAUNOAM, Dy. Secy.

S.O. 1790.—-The following draft of a Scheme further to amend 
various Dock Workers (Regulation of Fmployment) Schemes 
specified in the Schedule annexed beicto, which the Central 
Government pi oposes to make in exci eise of the powei s con¬ 
ferred by sub-section (1) of section 4 of the Dock Y'orkci s (Reg- 
lation of Employment) Act, 1948 (9 of 1948), is hereby published 
as required by the said sub-scction for the information of al 
person likely to be affected thereby and notice is hereby given 
that the said darft will be taken into consideration on or after 
the expiry of a period of forty-fisc days fiom the date of 
publication of this notification in the Official Gazette; 

Any objections or suggestions which may be received from 
any person with respect to said draft bcfoi e the aforesaid period 
will be taken into consideration by the Central Government. 

DRAFT SCHEME 

1. (1) This Scheme may be called the Dock Workers (Regu¬ 
lation of Employment) (Amendment) Scheme, 1982. 

(2) It shall oomo into force on the date of its final publica¬ 
tion in the Official Gazette.

4. It is stated that the minimum eainings of the loaders

the concerned workmen were getting much higher wages on

But when they were taken as Security Guards the management

Rs, 338- per month which was their minimum basic earnings. 
It is also stated that the scale of pay of Security Guai d vi4iich 
are in Grade G is Rs. 285-.360/- per mouth as per N.C.W.

from the date of joining as Security Guards.

The following dispute has been refejred to this Tribunal foi 
adjudication by the^Ministry of Labour, Government of India, 
vide it's Notification No. L-22012(30')/79-D, 1V(B) dated 
30th July, 1980 

“Whether the action of the management of WestcinCoaT 
fields Limited, Kanhan Area in ielation to thcii 
Arabara Colliery in disnrssing S/Shri Shahabir, Ram 
brij and Rambriksh, fub-loaderg or Kastuiba Incline 
of Ambnra Colliery is justified ? If not, to what 
relief arc the concerned workmen entitled ?” 


2. The statement of the workmen is that the management 
served false charge-sheet against them in respect of certain 
incident on the night intervening 18th and 19th Septembei, 
1978; that they were placed undei suspension; that there was 
a BO called et^iiiry against them; that the said enquiry was 
without observing piinciples of natural justice and the pres¬ 
cribed rules and regulations; and that as a result of in invalid 
enquiry against them their ^rvice were terminated. 

3. The reply of the management is that all the aforesaid 
workmen were woriking as leaders in the Arnbara Colliery and 
during the third shift in the night intervening 18th and 19th 
September, 1978 they assaulted a Store keeper, Shri Rhetar 
Lai, while on duty; that m a result of this assault he was bea¬ 
ten to unconciousness and threatened to be killed him also, 
■^en this incident was brought to the notice of the manage- 
iment all the thi ee workmen wore asked to submit their replies 
and as replies were not satisfactory they were charge sheeted 
vide Ex. M/13, Ex. M/14 and Ex. M/15 on 22-9-1978. 
An enquiry was held against them and as a result of enquiry 
the imtjugned order of tentiinat'on of service was passed 
against all the three workmen. 

4. As the validity of the enquiry was chullcnged by the 
workmen a number of issues W'erc raised on 7-10-1980. 
While evidence was being recorded with regard to the validity 
of enquiry it was found that the enquiry was not validity 
conducted. The management was therefore peimitted to 
prove the charges against the woikmen by ev7dcnce before 
this Tribunal. Accordingly, management examined M.W. 2 
Shri Khetar Lai, M.W. 3, Shri Yogender, M.W, 4, Shri 
Jharoo Lai. M.W 5, D.-. D.K. Banerjee, M.W. 6, Shri 
Syd. Ramzan Ali and Shri Pannalal, M.W. 7, Documentary 
evidence were aKo tendered in suppoU of the charges. On 
behalf of the workmen only the workman, Shri Shahvir, 
M.W. 1, and Dr K. N. Datta W.W. 2 were examined. The 
other two members of the wwkmen did not step to witness 
box to give their evidence. 

5 In view of the order passed On .1-1-1981 the only 
issues that survive for decision aic Lsties Nos 7, 8 and 9 
ns reproduced below ■ 

Issue No. 7 :—Whether the punishment aw.irdcl to 
the delinquents is appropriate 7 

Issue No. 2 '—In case the enquiry proceeding arc held 
to be vitiated whether the deliquents are guilty of 
the misconduct for which they were ebarge- 
sheeted by the management ? 

Issue No 9 —Relief and costs 

T have considered the ora] and dociimentaiy evidence given 
by both the parties My findings on those issues ne ns 
under - 


Issue No. 8 ;—The workmen are proved to have 
been guilty of misconduct for which they were 
charge-sheeted by the management. 

Issue No, 7 The punishment awarded to the work 
men was appropriate and fully justlllod. 

Issue No. 9 :—The workmen arc not entitled to any 
relief ? 

REASONS 

6 The charge-, against the workmen vide Charge-Sheets 
Fx. M|1J, MH4 and M/15 were that .dl of them on the 
third shift on 18-9-1978 physically assaulted, abused and 
attempted to kill the co-worker M.W. 2, Kheter Lai. This 
action on the part of all the three workmen was in viola¬ 
tion of Cause 17 of the Certified ‘'tending Orders, The 
management h.is, as alieady afoiesaid, given oral evidence in 
suppoil of the charges The statement of M.W. 2 Shii 
KhcUir Lai, is that at about 2 or 2.30 a.m. while he was 
in the night shift in picking the stones the thiec workmen, 
SlShii Shahvir, Rambrij and Rambriksh complained that 
he was earning wages without working; that immediatalv 
thereafter he was subjected to abuses and was caught 
hold of by Shii Shahvir and made to fell down on the 
ground by the other two workmen. His mouth was then 
closed and it was only after he came to consciousness that 
he reported the mutter to the police. According to him, lie 
was sent to the hospital and was heated for about 15 days. 
Ex. M|16 is his complaint to the management which was 
prepared by M.W. 4, Shri Jharoo Lai, M.W. 3, Shri 

Yogender, sayis that while he was on du/y at the place of 

incident at some distance he heaid M.W. 2, Shii Khetar Lai, 
sherting for help. He then repoiteq to the higher authorities 
and took him to the police station with an Ambulance. 
M.W, 4, Shri Jharoo Lai also support M.W. 2, Shri Khetar 
T al, legarcling the assault by the workmen M.W. 6. Shri 

Syd. Ramzan Ali, Overman in the mine, says that on 

being informed about Shii Khetai Lai nnconscicusness he went 
to the spoil and found that Shii Khetar Lai was 
unconscious, At that time he was infonned by Shii Jharoo 
Lai that the aforesaid workmen had assaulted him. He then 
brought them on the surface when the workmen were 
named by Shri Khetar Lai a,s the assailants. He was then 
i.aken to the hospital and to tbe polict station. MW. 7, 
Shri Panna Lai, also implicates the workmen with the 
assault of Shri Kheter I>al which be claims to have wit¬ 
nessed after he rushed to the spot on nries for help being 
raised by Shri Khetar Lai. Last witness examined by the 
management is Dr. D K. Banerjee (M.W, 5) of the Regional 
Hospital at the Tiinnurdeo This hospital is under the Central 
(Jevernment. He had cMniuitJ Shri Kheter Lai 
on 20th .September, 1978 and found that he had some 
bruised on body and complained of some pain in body 
and knee joints. He was admitted in hospital on 20lh 
.September, 1978 at 6s p m and discharged on 4-10-1978. 
This injury, according to the Doctor, was of about 48 
hours duration and could have caused by blows As against 
this evidence given by the manage,pent rnly the workmen 
W.W. 1, Shii Shahvir, has given a complete danial to the 
evidence given by the management’s witnesses W.W. 2, 
Dr. K N. Datta, who was to first examine Shri Khetar Lai. 
has stated that he examined Shri JChetar I al on 19-9-78 
nt the Priinarv Health Clinic (PH.CJ ,it Tiinnurdeo when 
Shri Khetar Lai complained of pain on the chest but 
there was no mark of infun'. 

7. Commenting upon the evidence given by the manage¬ 
ment’s witnesse.s it was urged that all the aforesaid wit¬ 
nesses except M.W, 2, Shri Khetar Lai, during their statc- 
nienfs in the enauirv proceedings and also in ciiminul 
case instituted against the workmen have professed ignorance 
about the cause of M M 2, Shri Kheter I.al, receiving any 
injury. These witnesses, according to the workmen, were 
tutored witnesses put up by the management foi the first 
time before this Tribunal to aive false and fabricated evidence 
against about an incident which in fact did not take place. It 
was further urged that if the workman, Shri Kheter Lai, 
had been suhiected to anv assault or iniuiy, us urged by him. 
then there was no reason as to whv there should have been 
no mark of iniury on h^s person when he was examined bv 
Dr. Datta, W.W 2 on the following dav These witnesses 
when confronted with their previous statements have explained 
their omiiwlons in the previous statements by saying that 



1992 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 15. 1982/VAISAKHA. 25 1904 


[Pari It—S ec. 3(ii)] 


iincc the deliquent workmen hnd thieitened them with serious 
consequences in case they were to ninke any statement against 
them, they did not implicate these workmen wheie they 
were esamined by the enquiiy Ollicer as also in the criminal 
case, It was also urged that in a criminal case not only these 
three workmen but several other also were accused and it 
was only because of the acquittal of all the accused that these 
three workmen were chosen and charge-sheeted. I have con¬ 
sidered those contentions and find that though these conten¬ 
tions are arguable hut they are not at all tenable, 

B. The fact that duting the cxtiminfltioii of Shii Khetev Lai 
on 20th September, 1978 Dr, Banerji of the Central Govern¬ 
ment Regional Hospital at Junnardeo found injuriee on the 
pet son of Shii Khcler Lai is fully borne cut from his evidence. 
It was for this reason that Dr. Banerji admitted Shri Khcter 
Lai as an indoor patient and discharged him on 4-10-1978. 
There is no particular reason as to why a responsible and 
senior Central Government employee Incharge of a Central 
Government Hospital should have given false evidence. No 
doubt Dr. Datta's evidence shows that he did not find any 
mark of external injury on the person of Shri Khetev Lai, 
but from his evidence I find that he made a superficial exami¬ 
nation of Shii Kheter l-al otherwise there was no valid reason 
for Dr. Banerji to admit the person in the hospital and treat 
him for about 15 days Tt was nowhere suggested to Shri 
Khctrr Lai that the injuiics noticed by Dr, Banerji were 
either self inflicted or were received by him in some other 
incident. Accordingly in my opinion, I find no valid reason 
as to why the evidence of Dr. Dntta should be preferred to 
the evidence given by Dr. Banerji. 

9. No doubt except M.W. 6, Shri Syd. Ramzan All, Over¬ 
man, the other management's Mtnesses did not implicate the 
workmen with any act of violence on two prevloua occasions 
VIZ. when they were examined in the domestic enquiry and in 
the criminal case also. The workmen and these witnesses arc 
admittedly employed in the same coal mine and the manage¬ 
ment's witnesses appear to be telling the truth that because of 
threats extended by the workmen they did not have the 
courage to say against them during the domestic enquiry and 
in the criminal case also. So far as the evidence of Shri 
Ramzan All M.W. 6, is concerned, it clearly shows that he 
immediately went to the spot and was told by Shii Kheter 
Lai, that these workmen had assaulted him. At that moment 
it was not suggested that someone had come and tutored Shri 
Kheter Lai to implicate these workmen instead of the rcnl 
culprits. The subsequent conduct on the part of Shri Khe.ter 
Lai, Shri Jharoo Lai, Shri Ramzan Ali and others clearly 
established that there was an incident of assault and these 
workmen were first named ai> fhe .issaihitils. I do not there¬ 
fore attach any particular importance to the omission on the 
port of these workmen to implicate the workmen both in 
the domestic enquiry as well as in the criminal case. 

10. It was not established in the crose-examination of any 
of ‘these witnesses that they bad any particular reason to 
falsely implicate the workmen. The chain of circumstances 
which is established from the evidence of the aforesaid wit¬ 
nesses closely establishes that there was an incident of assault 
in which these three workmen were assailants andjM.W. 2, 
Shri Kheter I al was the victim. 


11, From the evidence which I have discussed and believed 
above, it is found that during the night shift when Shri 
Kheter Lai was working along with other workers as stone 
picker for no reason these three workmen assaulted him. 
No doubt that Shri Shabvir has pledged his oath to deny 
allegations made against him but the other two workmen have 
not entered Into the witness box and there is no satisfactory 
reason about their absence. The woiknien working in the 
coal mine and that too at odd hours in the under ground 
are expected to observe a code of conduct and give all help 
to their co-workmen. If Shri Khetar Lai was exhibiting delin¬ 
quency in the discharge of his duties they could have renorted 
the matter to the Overman, Shri Ramzan Ali or to the Mining 
Sirdat on duty. They, however, tried to teach a lesson to the 
the Workman by heating him to unconsciousness so much so 
that he has to he earned on the surficc fiom where he was 
to be taken to hospital and was treated for about 15 days. 
Such a conduct on the part of the workmen working under¬ 
ground has to seriously noticed. 


12. The management finding the chaigcs proved imposed 
the pimishment of dismissal from service of the aforesaid 
thice workmen. The management by leading evidence before 
this Tribunal has established the charges which in my opinion 
arc a very serious nature. They have been awarded the piinish- 
niciH ol dismiss 1 1 from scivicc. The punishment, in my 
opinion, is quite proportionate the gravity of the offence 
commited by the workmen. I, therefore, find that in this 
case the workman have been found to be guilty of serious 
misconduct and that the punishment of dismissal of service 
awarded to them was quite pioportionatc. The workmen 
accordingly are not entitled to any relief in these proceedings, 

13. As a lesult of the discussion and ilie reasons given 
above, 1 hold that the management of the Western Coalfields 
I united Kanhar Area was justified in dismissing the workmen 
S/Shri Sahavir, Rambrij and Ramhriksh, Tub-loaders on the 
ground of serious mscondiict i.e. assault on Shri Kheter Lai, 
workman working on the underground of the coal mine. The 
woikmen in these circumstances are not entitled to any 
relief. I leave both the parties to hear their own costs as 
imiiired in these proceedings.

Asstt. Manager, Shri A. K. Das, while on duty and threatcB- 
ipg him to death with abusive language was satisfactorily 
proved. The Enquiry Officer considered the medical evidence 
which corroborated the factum of assault on the Mlne^ 
Officer. A perusal of the record of enquiry Ex. M/8 and the 
repoi t Ex. M/7 will show that the evidence has been dis¬ 
cussed at length and the appreciation of the evidence is 
reasonable. 

9, In the light of the aforesaid evidence which remains 
unrebutted I am clearly of the view that the charge sheet 
Ex. M/1 was proved and the Enquiry Officer’s report is 
based on a proper appreciation of_ evidence. There is no 
invalidity in the mode of the enquiry held by the Enquiry 
Officer. The Enquiry Officer was from another mine and 
Iheie is no allegation that he had any bias against the tvork- 
tnan. Consequently, in the absence of any reliable evidence 
It has to be concluded that there was a valid and proper 
enquiry against the workman and the mine management was 
justified in accepting the report of the Enquiry Officer. 

10 In view of the proved charge of assault by the workn 
man on a superior officer with threats in abusive language 
for causing to death the management was Justified in im¬ 
posing punishment of dismissal from service. 

11. In the light of the view that I have taken above with 
regard to the validity of the enquiry it has to be held that 
the order of dismisal passed by the management was fuUy 
iustified and the workman is not entitled to any relief. A-waxd 
is passed accordingly against the workman and in favour of 
the management. Thfcre will bo no order as to costs of 
these proceedings. 

S. R. WAS, Presiding Officer

Mr. 1. D Lai for the workman in support of the case 
of the workman raises a pomt of law on the basis of which 
■he urges that even- assummg that the workman was doing 
boring work under contract the woiknian is entitled to the 
relief claimed by him. Accor ting to Mr. Eal it is admitted 
case of the manhgcnient that the concerned workman is a 
boring contractor in. Mohanpur colliery. The case of the 
management is that the workman does the boring work 
and is paid according to volume of work done^ by him. 
T'hexefore according to Mr. Lai relationship of employer 
and employee, should be taken to have been established bet¬ 
ween the parties. Reliance is piacd by Mi'. Lai in a decision 
reported- in 3 SCLJ. 2022 (Dhrangadbra Cheinical Works 
LW V. State of Saurashtra). According to the decision the 
test for determination of relationship between master and 
servant is tlje existence of the right in the master to super¬ 
vise and control the work done by the servant not only in 
th^ mutter of directing what work the seivanr is to do but 
also the manner in which he shall do bis \vork. brdinurUy a 
distinction is alwayis-'drawn between a contract for service 
and P confrarvt bf service. The correct method of lipproach 
would be to consider whether having regard lo the nature 
of work there was due control and supervision by the em¬ 
ployer. The hroMk distinction between a workman end an 
independent contractor is that while former agiecs to work, 
the latter agrees to get others work. Though a person who 
agiTccs himself to work dobs so work and is therefore a work¬ 
man he does not cease to be such a person by reason merely 
of tlte fact that he get other person to work’alongwith him. 
• Judging from this standard I must say that the workman in 
the prc,sent case has established that he was doing the work 
of boring in such way which had established a lelalionship 
of employee and employer between him and the manage¬ 
ment. 

. .7. In the result I hold that the action of the management 
.<n Mohanpiir Colliery in refusing to continue to employ 
Abdul Aziz. Boring Super\'isor, with effect from 27-3-74 
js not justified-and that the workman is entitled to rcinstote- 
ihent. Since in the case before me tho workman concerned 
wai not Siting idle from the date h© was refused pemiisslon 
to resume duty. I hold that he is not entitled lo back wages. 
But ho is entitled to reinstatement from the day he reports 
for duty within a month from the date of publication of award. 
'The rcfarhhcc is answered aceordingiv. Parties are to bear

This is an application which purports to be a complaint 
under Sec. 33-A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, made 
by the above named workman on the following allocations :_ 

That the applicant is working since 14-7-1970 in the Sub- 
Dwtsio^ Telegraph Officer, Post and Telegraph Depart¬

issuel by the Department on 14-4-1978; that the applicant 
sent several representations for his regular appointment but 
tne department has not issued any order appointing him in

In the Ujjain Division, but the applicant whose name was 
mentioned at SI. No. 13 has not been appointed and his jmri-

practice and against the principles of natural justice. The 
applicant further contends that such undue appointments of

to him, pe BMhcant. therefore, prays that the directions may

3. From the aforesaid pleadings of the parties, it appears 
that the appHoant/complmnant has submitted an application 
‘leforc the Conciliation Officer (Central) Indore and the same 
s pending before bim .The question, therefore, arises whether 
his application is maintainable before this Tribunal under 

Sec. 33-A of the Industrial Disputes Act, Arguments on this 
question were heard on 24-3-1982. 

4. My finding on the aforesaid question is that the applica¬ 
tion is not maintainable for the following reasons 


Neither in the allegations made by the oppHcant in his 
applfcation nor in arguments advanced by the Counsel for 
the applicant, it is contended that either with regard to him¬ 
self or for any oher workman a dispute is pending before 
this Tribunal. A complaint under Sec. 33-A can only be 
made before this Tribunal when there is a pendentdlte 
dispute before this Tribunal in which the applicant/compfetaant 
being a workman of the department is connected with the 
dispute. The applicant should have made this application/com¬ 
plaint before the authority before whom a dispute in which 
fie is connected with was pending raised an industrial dis¬ 
pute under Sec. 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act. Therefore 
the applicantlcomplainant has failed to show that there is a 
dispute pendhig before this Tribunal and he is either a party 
to it or if in any nmy cbnnected with that dispirte, In these 
clfcnrastance®, the apNication fails and I hold thnt this Tri¬ 
bunal has no Jurisdiction to deal with the present application! 
eomplaint made by the applicant under Sec. 33-A of the 
Industrial Disputes Act. The application is accordingly dis- 
mlMcd as not maintainable. Both the parlies are directed to 
bear their own costs as incurred.

New Delhi, the 1st May, 1982 

S.O. 18M,—Whereas the Central Government having been 
satisfied that the public interest so required had, in pursuance 
of the' provision of sub-clarac (vi) of clause (nj of section 2 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), declared 
by the notification of the Goveinment of India in the Ministry 
of Labour No. S.O. 3003 dated the 14th October, 1981, the 
service in any oil field to be a public utility service for the 
purposes of the said 7\ct, for a period of six months, from 
the 10th November, 1981; 

And whereas, the Central Government is of opinion that 
public interest requires the extension of the said period by a 
further period of six months ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
the proviso to sub-clause (vi) of clause (n) of sectiwi 2 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), Central Gov¬ 
ernment hereby declares the said industry to be a public 
utility service for the purposes of the said Act for a further 
period of six months frotii the 10th May, 1982. 

[No S. 11017/6/81-D.I.A.l 
L. K, NARAYANAN, Under Secy.
